- Open item 239 (2026-10-22): O58's look cannot be taken on TEST. O58 removes `config/catalogs/meta.yml`, so the Settings view's Catalogs group should no longer list it. The Settings view is a unique view: on TEST, opening it brings up the Settings tab your System page holds, with your filter `opacity` set, which hides the Catalogs group. (a) I move that Settings tab to TEST, clear the filter, take the look, then type `opacity` back and move the tab back to the System page. Pro: the look is taken now, and your page ends as it was. Con: your System page is changed for a minute. (b) the look stays owed (`agents/reviews/owed-looks.md`) and is taken when you next close or clear that Settings tab. Pro: your page is not touched. Con: the look waits on you. Recommended: (a); (b) is the lazy option. O58 lands now either way, with the look named as not taken. Ruled (a) by Axel, 2026-10-22; booked in plan.md Rulings.
- Open item 238 (2026-10-22): O54, your saved tabs. Oculus saves your open tabs in `~/.oculus/layouts.json` and `views.json`, and a tab that shows a record stores its id there, today the bare key for the primary hub's record (your EEG-sAAD tab holds `"id": "eeg-saad-challenge"`) and `<hub>:<key>` for another hub's. Ruling 236 makes every internal id `<hub>:<key>`, and ruling No hub prefix shown forbids writing that joined id to a file, so after O54 a tab stores the hub and the key as two fields. The no-migration rule forbids code that reads the old form. (a) at O54's landing the orchestrator changes those two files once, adding the hub to every record tab, so every tab opens its record as before. Pro: no tab lost; no code carries the old form. Con: one write by hand to files in `~/.oculus` while your server runs, made when the page holds no unsaved layout. (b) leave the files: after O54 every saved record tab opens as not found and you open those records again. Pro: nothing to do. Con: every record tab of every saved page is lost. Recommended: (a); (b) is the lazy option. O54 waits for O50 to land, so nothing is built before you rule. Ruled (a) by Axel, 2026-10-22; booked in plan.md Rulings.
- Open item 237 (2026-10-22): O54's draft brief on disk, a clash between the `spec` skill and the ban on `git restore`. `agents/briefs/O54.md` holds the brief written before rulings 231 to 236, uncommitted. The `spec` skill refuses a step whose brief path holds an uncommitted change (`~/.claude/skills/spec/SKILL.md:88`), and the way it gives to clear it, `git restore` (`SKILL.md:452`), is banned. (a) commit the draft as it stands, as a ledger record of O54 marked superseded, so the path is clean; the next `/spec O54` writes the new brief over it under rulings 203, 231 to 236 and No hub prefix shown. Pro: no git restore, nothing lost, `/spec` runs as written. Con: a superseded brief is in one commit of the history. (b) delete the file and let `/spec` write a fresh one. Con: the draft is lost from the ledger (a copy stays in the session scratchpad only). Recommended: (a); (b) is the lazy option. Ruled by Axel, 2026-10-22: neither option; the brief is restored to main's copy with `git restore`, as the `spec` skill says, and `git restore` is allowed again.
- Open item 235 (2026-10-22): O54's tests have no file they may live in. The plan's rule (`agents/spec.md:59`): "A test exists only as the test of one entry of `agents/guarded.md`, and `agents/guarded.md` names it there". `grep -c "hub-portfolio\|hub-routing\|hub-undo" agents/guarded.md` prints 0, so `tests/data/hubs/hub-portfolio.test.ts`, `tests/routes/hubs/hub-routing.test.ts` and `hub-undo.test.ts` are not named, and O54's cases (for example "main and lab both hold `alpha`: the ids are `main:alpha` and `lab:alpha`, and no page shows either joined id") have no test file. (a) name `hub-portfolio.test.ts` under G32 (what the hub shows is what its files say) and the other two under G8 (each write kind changes only the file and key it names), and O54's cases become test rows there. (b) O54's cases are checked by reading only, so nothing fails when the behaviour later breaks. Recommended: (a); (b) is the lazy option. - Open item 234 (2026-10-22): O54, the Audit reports a missing venue the portfolio has. The Audit checks each hub on that hub's own build, before the hubs are merged (`src/lib/server/root/watch/portfolio.ts:749-760`, `projects: Object.values(part.snapshot.entities.projects)`). Lab's project with `venue_id: nature`, where only main holds `nature`, loses its venue in lab's own build (`src/lib/data/snapshot/normalise.ts:730`, `delete item.venueId`), and the Audit rule `gaps` then reports "no venue" (`src/lib/server/content/audit/projects.ts:206-216`, `audit.msg_no_venue`), though the portfolio resolves it to main's `nature`. (a) the Audit judges each hub's records with their references resolved across the hubs, as the portfolio does. (b) leave it: every reference across hubs gives a false "no venue". Recommended: (a); (b) is the lazy option. Not an option but required by ruling No hub prefix shown either way: the Audit view today shows a finding's scope as `lab:alpha` (`Audit.svelte:137` shows `finding.scope`, which `rules.ts:282` builds as `<hub>:<scope>`); it shows `alpha` with the hub chip. Ruled (a) by Axel, 2026-10-22; booked in plan.md Rulings; O54's line widened.
- Open item 233 (2026-10-22): O54, a rename does not reach the other hubs. Renaming a key rewrites every reference to it, but the references it rewrites come only from the files of the same hub: `graphOf` keeps `scan.file.root === root` (`src/lib/data/snapshot/references/lookup.ts:93-96`). Example: you rename main's venue `nature` to `nature-mag`; lab's project `venue_id: nature`, which ruling 203 resolved to main's `nature`, is not rewritten, so it then names nothing (a finding), or, when another hub holds a `nature`, silently points at that other record. (a) a rename rewrites the references in every hub that resolve to the renamed record. (b) a rename rewrites its own hub only. Recommended: (a); (b) is the lazy option. Ruled (a) by Axel, 2026-10-22; booked in plan.md Rulings; O54's line widened.
- Open item 232 (2026-10-22): O54, a form's dropdown offers only the record's own hub. In a record's Edit form, the dropdown of a field like `venue_id` lists only the records of the record's own hub (`entityChoices`, `src/lib/state/forms/schema.ts:198-215`, `.filter((id) => rootOf(entities[id], primary) === root)`). Under ruling 203 lab's project may point at main's `nature`, but the form never offers it, so such a reference can only be typed into the file by hand. (a) the dropdown lists the own hub's records first, then the other hubs' records, each with its hub chip; picking one writes the bare key (`nature`), which resolves own hub first to that record; when the own hub also holds a record with that key, only the own hub's is offered, because the bare key always resolves to it. (b) own hub only. Recommended: (a); (b) is the lazy option. Ruled (a) by Axel, 2026-10-22; booked in plan.md Rulings; O54's line widened.
- Open item 231 (2026-10-22): O54, the YAML editor disagrees with the Findings view. When a hub's file is open in the YAML editor, a field that names a record (such as `venue_id`) is checked against a list of allowed keys, `enum: [...keys, null]` (`src/lib/data/snapshot/schema/record.ts:307`), and that list is built per hub (`scopedSchemas`, `src/lib/data/snapshot/schema/set.ts:87-102`), so it holds the hub's own keys only. Example: lab's project has `venue_id: nature` and only main holds `nature`. Under ruling 203 the portfolio resolves it to main's `nature` and the Findings view reports nothing, but the editor underlines `nature` as not allowed. (a) the editor's list also holds the keys of the other hubs, so the editor and the Findings view agree. (b) leave it: the editor marks as wrong what the portfolio accepts. Recommended: (a); (b) is the lazy option. Ruled (a) by Axel, 2026-10-22; booked in plan.md Rulings; O54's line widened.
- Open item 236 (2026-10-22): O54, reopen ruling 223. Under 223 (b) a non-primary record's id is bare or `<hub>:<key>` depending on whether another hub holds the same key, and the primary's is always bare; that dependence is the cause of open items 228, 229 and 230, and of the hub being guessed wrongly from a bare id (`hooks.ts` `recoverPlaces`, `tones.ts` `domainEntry`). (a) every record's internal id is always `<hub>:<key>`, the primary's included, whatever the other hubs hold; files, forms and findings use the bare key, references resolve own hub first, and under ruling No hub prefix shown the joined id is never written or shown: a page shows the key or the record's label with its hub chip. Pro: an id never changes with the workspace, so 228, 229 and 230 fall away and the `id_prefix` check (`merge.ts` `ambiguousIds`) is no longer needed; the hub of every record is read from its id. Con: every place that takes the primary's ids as bare changes, and the stored primary ids (saved tabs, page states) change once. (b) keep 223 (b) and rule 228, 229 and 230 one by one. Con: three extra parts that only exist to patch an id that moves. Recommended: (a); (b) is the lazy option. 231 to 235 stand either way. Ruled (a) by Axel, 2026-10-22; booked in plan.md Rulings; O54's line rewritten.
- Open item 228 (2026-10-22): O54, an id that changes when the workspace changes. Under ruling 223 a record's id depends on the other hubs: lab's only `x` is `x`, and becomes `lab:x` once the primary or another hub of the workspace holds an `x` too, and back again when that hub leaves. A tab, a page's saved state, a Focus row or an address that stored `x` then opens the primary's `x`, another record, with no sign. (a) a stored reference to a record holds its hub with its key (the layout and page states store both), so it opens the same record whatever its prefix, or says not found when that hub is gone. Pro: a tab never opens a different record. Con: the stored page and tab states change shape (the layout file under `~/.oculus` included), one more part in O54. (b) as the ids give it. Pro: no change. Con: after a workspace change a tab can show another hub's record as if it were the one you opened. Recommended: (a); (b) is the lazy option. Closed 2026-10-22 by ruling 236 (a): an id no longer depends on what the other hubs hold, so the case cannot arise.

Closed 2026-10-22 by ruling 236 (a): an id no longer depends on what the other hubs hold, so the case cannot arise.
- Open item 230 (2026-10-22): O54, a key that looks like a prefixed id. Under ruling 223 lab's bare key `team:x` collides with team's prefixed `team:x` and one is dropped. (a) the finding covers such a key of any hub, and neither record is dropped silently. Closed 2026-10-22 by ruling 236 (a): an id no longer depends on what the other hubs hold, so the case cannot arise.
- Open item 227 (2026-10-22): O50, a rename of a person key carried to `settings.self`. Open item 226 said that adding `people` to the reference index's option sources (`OPTION_SOURCES`) makes a rename rewrite `settings.self`; that was wrong. The index reads oculus's own defaults files and the hub's data files, never a workspace file (`optionFiles`, `src/lib/data/sources/config/catalog/schemas/references.ts:119-127`, read only through `engineScans` in `src/lib/data/snapshot/references/build.ts:187`; `buildPortfolioWithSchemas` hands it no workspace file, `src/lib/data/index.ts:519-529`), so the row alone adds one place, the empty `self: ''` of `config/defaults/sources.yml`, and a rename of your person key still leaves `settings.self` naming the old key. O50's builder found it on its first run of C14. (a) O50 also makes the reference index read the open workspace file's `settings:` as an options file (its path passed from `src/lib/server/root/watch/portfolio.ts` through `buildPortfolioWithSchemas` to one more scan in `build.ts`), with the `OPTION_SOURCES` row. Pro: renaming your person key keeps you marked, which is what 226 (a) was for, and a workspace-file option drawn from `domains` or `clusters` is rewritten by a rename too. Con: three more paths in O50. (b) only the row, as 226 (a) was worded. Pro: one line. Con: it changes nothing you can see. Recommended: (a); (b) is the lazy option. O50 builds every other part meanwhile and leaves the row and C14 until you rule. Ruled (a) by Axel, 2026-10-22; sent to O50's builder during its build.
- Open item 226 (2026-10-22): O50, who the user is as the workspace setting `self`. O50's brief check found that oculus's rename of a key rewrites only the option sources its reference index knows (`OPTION_SOURCES`, `src/lib/data/sources/config/catalog/schemas/references.ts:26-33`: `domains`, `clusters` and four vocabulary words). Today renaming your own person key keeps you marked, since the mark is `self: true` on the entry; once O50 moves the mark to `settings.self`, a rename leaves the setting naming a key that no longer exists, so no person is marked and the check reports it. Carrying the rename is not in O50's line, so it is yours (ruling Brief check scope). (a) O50 adds `people` to `OPTION_SOURCES`, so a rename of a person key rewrites `settings.self` as it rewrites an option drawn from `domains`; one line and one path added. Pro: renaming your key keeps you marked, as today. Con: one more path in O50. (b) as O50's brief stands: the rename leaves `settings.self` stale and the check's finding tells you to fix it by hand. Pro: O50 stays on its paths. Con: a behaviour that works today stops working. Recommended: (a); (b) is the lazy option. O50 is prepared without it; on (a) it goes into O50's brief before its builder starts if the ruling comes first, otherwise into its repair round. Ruled (a) by Axel, 2026-10-22; booked while O50's builder was running, so the change is sent to it before its report.
- Open item 225 (2026-10-22): O52, the Focus board's headings in one shape. O52's brief check found that a career file with no title, no next milestone and no host in flight (the normaliser allows it: a half-filled career file draws the half it has) leaves the career banner's line empty, so its fold control is named "Unfold " with nothing after it, the name a screen reader reads and the tooltip shows. Naming it needs a heading word the text catalog does not have (`config/text/views/boards/focus.yml` holds no career heading), which widens O52 beyond its line, so it is yours (ruling Brief check scope). (a) O52 adds the key `focus.career_heading: Career strategy` and names the fold control "Fold Career strategy" / "Unfold Career strategy", the row's title staying the career line; the path `config/text/views/boards/focus.yml` is added. (b) the control keeps the career line as its name and nothing is added. Pro: O52 stays on its paths. Con: an empty career file gives a control named "Unfold ". Recommended: (a), since it ends the nameless control; (b) is the lazy option. O52 is built now on its line; on (a) the key goes into O52's repair round with the path widened. - Open item 223 (2026-10-22): O54, own hub first across the hubs of the workspace (ruling 203 (b)). The merged portfolio keeps each kind of record in a table keyed by its id (`src/lib/data/snapshot/hubs/merge.ts`, `entities[kind]` built with `byId`), and a record of a hub other than the primary is kept apart today only because every one of its ids carries the hub's prefix (`entitiesOf`, `prefixer`). Ruling 203 (b) gives the prefix to a project id held by two hubs and to no other id, so when two hubs both hold `smith` in people, `nature` in venues, a collection `grants` or an entry key, the two records have one id and the table keeps only one of them; the ruling does not say how the other is kept. The same choice decides whether the primary's copy of a shared key changes its id. (a) every key two hubs both hold (a project, a registry entry, a collection, a collection entry, a lead) carries its hub's prefix in the merged portfolio, both copies and the primary's among them; a key one hub holds stays bare. Pro: one rule for every kind; a reference still resolves own hub first. Con: when a hub holding the same key is opened beside the primary, the primary's id changes, so what names it by id (a tab's state, a Focus row) finds nothing while that hub is open. (b) as (a), but the primary's copy keeps its bare id and only the other hubs' copies carry their prefix. Pro: the primary's ids never change, as today, where the primary's records carry no `root`; Con: the primary is treated apart from the other hubs. (c) no id carries a prefix and every table of the portfolio is keyed by hub and id. Pro: the ruling's words hold as written. Con: every lookup, relation table, page and tab state that names a record by id must name its hub too, a new wire shape and a far larger step. Recommended: (b), since the primary's ids stay as they are and every other copy is told apart by the prefix the ruling already gives projects. (c) is the larger; (b) is recommended for its result, not its cost, and no option leaves work undone. The step waits for the ruling; nothing of O54 is built. Ruled (b) by Axel, 2026-10-22; O54 rewritten.
- Open item 224 (2026-10-22): O62, the removal of the unread vocabulary key `archive_order`. - Open item 219 (2026-10-22): which stage words are live and which are done. Today only the type `active` carries `live`, so a `submitted`, `in-prep` or `exploring` project is not in flight for the Focus backlog, the venue concentration count (`state/sources/registries/venues.ts`), the Map and the audit's in-flight rules; and the types `finished` and `archived` carry no flag, so under ruling 213 a finished project would stay on Projects. After ruling 218 every stage word is a type and needs one of these. (a) idea (the `idea` flag, not live, not done): idea, nascent, scoping; live: de-risking, exploring, in-prep, building, running, active, submit-ready, submitted, revision, accepted; done (`terminal`): published, finished, archived, nogo, dropped, disproved, abandoned; neither: paused. (b) as now. Recommended: (a). Ruled 2026-10-22 (a), see plan.md Rulings.

- Open item 213 (2026-10-22, redone): how a board selects a project by its state. A project's state is held in four kinds of thing: its type word (one of 16), the flags of that type word (11: live, terminal, dead, archive_outcome, on_map, placed, standard, idea, public, negative, dead_end), its status chips (any of 33) and the flags of those chips (17, with ruling 216's name: chip, closes_deadline, deadline_met, live_subtasks, waiting, blocked, pauses_countdown, submit_ready, published, acknowledged, lead_closed, lead_declined, lead_applied, default_lead, default_application, default_task, new_idea; of these only chip, closes_deadline, deadline_met, waiting, blocked, pauses_countdown, submit_ready, published and new_idea are carried by chip words). (a) a board names a `type:` block and a `status:` block, each holding up to four lists: `words` (the project has any of them), `not_words` (it has none of them), `flags` (one of its words carries any of these flags), `not_flags` (none of its words carries any); every list the board names must hold; a word or flag that its vocabulary does not hold is a finding; a board group (ruling 215) takes the same two blocks. Archive: `type: { flags: [terminal] }`; Projects: `type: { not_flags: [terminal] }`; in review: `status: { words: [submitted, revision] }`; blocked: `status: { flags: [blocked] }`. (b) the same eight lists as flat keys: `type_words`, `type_not_words`, `type_flags`, `type_not_flags`, `status_words`, `status_not_words`, `status_flags`, `status_not_flags`. (c) words only, the four word lists: a selection by a flag is written out as the words that carry it, and a word that gains or loses the flag is changed on every board too. (d) flags only, the four flag lists: a selection by one word needs a flag made for it. (e) (a), and a board or a group may name `any:`, a list of `type:`/`status:` pairs of which one must hold, for a selection that takes either a type word or a status chip, as today's groups do (a group's `keys` match the type or a chip): Coauthor's in progress (submitted, in-prep, exploring) and the outputs groups of Coauthor and Archive (tech-report, software as type or chip) need it to keep what they take today. Recommended: (e) if open item 218 keeps both lists, (a) if 218 (a) gives each word one list; (a) alone drops the either-or of those three groups; (b) is (a) without the grouping; (c) and (d) leave out one kind. Ruled 2026-10-22 (a), see plan.md Rulings.
- Open item 218 (2026-10-22): the type and the status chips both hold a project's stage. 13 words are both a type and a chip (published, submitted, in-prep, paused, tech-report, software, nogo, dropped, disproved, abandoned, exploring, phd, finished), and other stage words are chips only (submit-ready, revision, accepted, nascent, scoping, de-risking, building, running), so a project can say its stage twice and say it differently; the audit has rules for that disagreement (a published chip on unfinished work, `audit/projects.ts:401-403`; a terminal type with a live chip). (a) each word has one home: the type is the project's stage, the one word every stage word belongs to (idea, nascent, scoping, de-risking, exploring, in-prep, building, running, active, submit-ready, submitted, revision, accepted, published, paused, finished, archived, nogo, dropped, disproved, abandoned), with the flags those words carry as chips moved onto them as type flags; the chips are labels that say nothing of the stage: the form of the output (journal, conference, thesis, abstract, poster, tech-report, software, phd) and the situation (blocked, results-positive, admin-prep, numbers-pending, ai-tells); `lead` and `coauthor` go by ruling 206; the audit rules that compare the type with the chips go; a manifest that carries a stage word as a chip is a finding, and oculus's shipped vocabulary and fixtures are rewritten once. - Open item 214 (2026-10-22): what in oculus's `config/` can be overridden, and where. Today a hub's `.oculus/config.yml` may replace the 24 keys of `HUB_LIST` (`data/helpers/hub-names.ts:329-354`); a workspace's `settings:` may set every other option of the five `defaults/` files; a hub's `local.yml` holds the machine keys. Nothing can change: in `vocab.yml` 15 of its 16 lists (all but `statuses`), the 7 other catalogs (`agents`, `file-nesting`, `glyphs`, `icons`, `latex`, `meta`, `unimath`), the 3 schemas (`manifest`, `deck`, `cpp`) and the interface words of `text/`. (a) each part of `config/` belongs to one place. The workspace, for how one person works: the five defaults files as today, `agents`, `file-nesting`, `latex`, `unimath`, `glyphs`, `icons` and the interface words (a language). Fixed: `config/options/`, which defines every setting and checks every override, the deck and cpp schemas, which describe files oculus's own code reads, (`meta.yml` goes as a defect, step O58: no code reads it). (b) everything overridable by the hub and by the workspace alike. (c) as now. Recommended: (a); (c) is the lazy option. Ruled 2026-10-22 (a), see plan.md Rulings.

- Open item 207 (2026-10-21): `lead:` and `collaborators:` on collection entries. (1) Every collection may declare the two fields in its `schema:`, each a list of person keys, and oculus reads them by those names. (2) A badge: today only a project carries one, the hand-set status chips `lead` ("first author") and `coauthor`; an entry or a card whose `lead:` holds `self` gets a Lead badge, derived. (3) A filter facet Role on every board and collection view: Lead, Collaborator. (4) A toggle Mine only, on by default; off, it also shows the entries whose `lead:` and `collaborators:` both lack `self`. (5) An entry or a project that names no one counts as the hub owner's (ruling Owner and self), so the owner's view is unchanged and another person's hides it by default. (6) The project boards take the same toggle and badge. Recommended: all six; leaving collections without the fields is the lazy option. Ruled 2026-10-21, see plan.md Rulings.
- Open item 208 (2026-10-21): setting `self` without editing YAML. (a) a Settings row (its key is open item 204) with a picker of the people registry, written to the open workspace file as every workspace setting is, and the Person page's "this is me" tag becomes a button that writes the same key; (b) the Settings row only; (c) the Person page button only. Recommended: (a). Ruled 2026-10-21, see plan.md Rulings.

- Open item 205 (2026-10-21, people relative to the user): people relative to the user. `collaborators:` on a project names the people besides the hub's owner, so it reads wrong for anyone else using the hub (a student, a coauthor). (a) leave it; (b) `collaborators:` becomes the whole team, the owner included, and the views show the team without the workspace's `self`; (c) a project names its `lead:` (a person key) beside `collaborators:`, the team is both, and the views show it without `self`. With it: whose hub it is (the portfolio chat's "research hub of {owner}") and who is looking (`self`, ruling Self in the workspace) are two facts; keep the owner as a person key in the hub's `config.yml` and `self` in the workspace, or one fact as now. Ruled: (c), with `lead:` a list of person keys, and the owner and `self` as two facts (Axel: "205 (c) seems nice, and maybe lead can be a list", "Agreed"); plan.md rulings "Open item 205" and "Owner and self", step O53.

- Open item 178 (2026-10-20, brats2020's Recover unpack): (a), O45 after O44, the key `folder` (Axel). brats2020's registry entry (`path: ~/data/BraTS2020_train`, a `kaggle` source with `unpack: true` and no `strip`) does not match the folder raw-pls reads. Kaggle sends the dataset as one zip (`sources/tokened.ts`), holding `BraTS2020_TrainingData/MICCAI_BraTS2020_TrainingData/` and `BraTS2020_ValidationData/MICCAI_BraTS2020_ValidationData/`, so a Recover unpack puts `name_mapping.csv` two folders down, where raw-pls (`hpc/run_all.sh:28`, `run_reg.sh:17`, `build_cache.slurm:18`, `build_grade_from_dir.py:37`) does not find it. `strip: 2` alone does not fix it: `place()` in `unpack.ts` drops the leading folders on every branch, so the validation cases land in the same folder, and `run_reg.sh:46` takes the first `*_t1.nii*` it finds as the registration template, which could then be a validation case. Nothing breaks today: the cluster's `BraTS2020_train/` already holds the training folder alone, and no other entry is affected. Options: (a) Recover's unpack gains a way to place one folder of the archive (a path inside the archive in place of a strip count): the `SourcePlace` field in `model-entities.ts`, its schema and check, the plan's unpack line, `place()`, tests, DESIGN and HELP, and brats2020's entry pointed at `BraTS2020_TrainingData/MICCAI_BraTS2020_TrainingData`; the validation folder in `~/.cache/kagglehub` deleted; (b) `strip: 1`, the folder holding both MICCAI folders, raw-pls's four places pointed at the training subfolder, and the cluster folder moved to match. Recommendation: (a), since raw-pls stays as it is and no data nobody reads is kept; (b) is the lazy option, which moves the mismatch into raw-pls.

- Open item 154 (2026-10-18, ruled in full 2026-10-19, O38's /spec, libraries and data folders): (1) Downloads on this Mac: (a) `node-downloader-helper` 2.1.11 (MIT, one maintainer, last release 2026-03-06, no dependencies) for resume and progress, the checksum computed over the written file; (b) written by hand on Node's `fetch` with range requests, the checksum computed while the bytes arrive. Recommended (b): the checksum and each file's state live in the same loop as the download, and the library would still need that loop around it; (a) is not lazier. (2) Unpacking on this Mac: (a) the system `tar` (bsdtar 3.5.3, libarchive, reads zip, tar.gz and 7z alike), no new dependency; (b) `yauzl` 3.4.0 for zip and `tar` 7.5.22 for tar.gz, with no maintained library for 7z (`node-7z` last released 2022), so isles24 would still need a system tool. Recommended (a). On the cluster the unpack job uses the cluster's `unzip`, `tar` and `7z`; whether `7z` exists on a compute node is not verified, the cluster being unreachable from here. (3) Folder names: 14 sets with data today live in a folder that is not their key (`~/data/BraTS2020` for brats2020, `~/data/PICAI` for picai, `~/data/ISLES24`, `~/data/ACDC`, `~/data/BraTS-METS`, `~/data/mne/...`, `~/data/linfoot` for linfoot-sim, `~/data/mimic-ecg` and `~/data/archive/...` for mimic-iv-ecg, `~/data/archive/ptb-xl-1.0.3.zip` for ptb-xl, and on this Mac `~/data/mit-bih` for mitdb). (a) `path:` is always `~/data/<key>`: each folder renamed on both machines and every project path that names it changed; (b) `path:` names the one folder the set has now, any name under `~/data`. Recommended (a), since the registry's own `key` field says the key is the folder name under `~/data`; (b) is the lazy option. (4) Unregistered data on this Mac: `~/data/fed-faithful-ecg-cache` (612 MB), `~/data/ecg-readable-substrate` (128 KB), `~/data/ms-lipid-handshake` (empty) and three `.py` files in `~/data` itself (`ecgfounder_net1d.py`, `hubert_dataset.py`, `hubert_ecg_inspect.py`); the hub rule wants every folder there registered. (a) O38 registers or removes each, on Axel's yes per path; (b) a step of its own, the lazy option. Recommended (a). Part 4 ruled (a) on 2026-10-19, each path on its own yes. Raised 2026-10-19 and open: for part 1 a third option, (c) `curl -C -` run as a separate process on both machines (on this Mac a detached process whose pid and log oculus keeps, on the cluster in tmux as designed), recommended over (a) and (b); for part 2 the multiplatform option `7z-wasm` 1.2.0 (7-Zip 24.09 in WebAssembly, LGPL-2.1 with the unRAR restriction, one maintainer, last release 2025-06-23), measured here on 5 GiB and 1 GiB archives, recommended; for part 3 the lock waits on the per-project update list, and `hccnet-mri` (`/deepstore/datasets/bms/hcc_study`, shared university storage) cannot follow `~/data/<key>`; the move of the hub data files into `.oculus/data`, flat `.oculus/data/<key>.yml` (oculus DESIGN.md:762) or the subfolders the user proposed, and whether it is part of O38. Ruled 2026-10-19: part 1 (c), part 2 `7z-wasm`, part 4 the six items as proposed, the data move inside O38 as option (c). Still open: the folder names under `.oculus/data`, and part 3 (whether the 14 folders are renamed to their keys, and the rule for `hccnet-mri` on shared storage). Ruled later on 2026-10-19: subject folders; no folder renamed and no rule that the key is the folder. Still open: whether joint-work and events go to `academic/` and whether pillars and domains get a folder of their own; whether the spread sets (mimic-iv-ecg, ptb-xl, brats-2023-synapse) are gathered into one folder each. ## Verification, every package

A check run by hand is read by its own exit (`npm run lint > out.txt 2>&1; echo $?`), never through a pipe into `tail`, whose exit is the pipe's; the landing script reads each step's own status. When the script stops on the git watcher's timing case, the file alone is the second measurement and the remaining steps run by hand in the script's order.

A landing runs the `land` skill's own script from the repository root, as that skill's section "The landing script" says: `sh ~/.claude/skills/land/templates/land.sh <state file> <slug>-<step> <base>`. It makes the worktree wip commit, cherry-picks the step's whole range onto main, runs the verify list of this file through the skill's `templates/checks.sh` and prints the diff stat and the staged paths for the booking; the package commit, the plan tick and the state rewrite stay the orchestrator's. The ledger holds no landing script of its own.

Until O1 lands there is no oculus toolchain; the cockpit's commands are the gate for the C packages.
- Cockpit packages (any bug fix on the frozen cockpit): `cd tools/cockpit && python3 build.py --selftest && python3 build.py --dummy && bash tests/run.sh`, all from `tools/cockpit`, then once from the hub root: `python3 tools/cockpit/build.py --selftest`.
- Oculus packages, from O1 on, run from the repository root: `npm test` (Vitest: boundaries, contrast, keys, integrity, per-source and per-server tests), `npm run check` (svelte-check), `npm run build` (the Node adapter build must complete), and from Q1 (2026-09-11) `npm run lint` (ESLint flat config, exit 0 with no output); from Q2 (2026-09-11) the orchestrator also runs `npm run build && npm run test:browser` at every landing (Playwright on the installed Chrome over a copy of `demo/`, 18 specs, about 25 s). O1 defines the first three scripts and the ledger names them per package after that.
- Oculus packages, from the repository root, in this order: `npm test -- --reporter=dot`, `npm run check`, `npm run build`, `npm run format:check`, `npm run lint`, `LC_ALL=C grep -rna --exclude=glyphs.yml '[^ -~]' src tests bin config` with empty output as the pass, `find src tests config bin -type f | xargs wc -l | sort -rn | head -3` (nothing over 1000 lines), then `npm run test:browser` (port 8792 free).
- Every package: `LC_ALL=C grep -rna '[^ -~]' src tests` from the repository root prints nothing (`-a` since 2026-09-11: two source files carried a raw NUL byte as a string separator, which BSD grep skipped as binary, so the gate was green over them), and the same over every other file the diff touches finds nothing new except `plan.md`'s checkmarks. `git status --short` from the repository root shows only the package's files.

- `tests/shell/workbench.test.ts` imports every view inside one case under vitest's 5 s timeout; in a full run, where the import competes with the other workers' transforms (import phase 73 to 105 s red against 32 to 49 s green, at load averages of 10 to 20 on the 11-core machine), that case can time out and take the file's later mounts with it, while the file alone is green in 3 s. It is read like the git burst case: the file alone is the second measurement, and the full run is repeated once no other vitest is running and the load is down; nothing is loosened. R12d's edge 6 hoists the import out of the timed case.

## Where things are

- Design: `DESIGN.md`. Its "Decisions taken" and "Still undecided" sections are the record; a new decision is appended there, dated.
- Ledger: `.scratch/migration/plan.md`, with a "Rulings" section for Axel's decisions. Agent material: `agents/spec.md` (the standing spec every oculus package runs under), `agents/briefs/<package>.md` (one per package, written by the orchestrator before dispatch, premises checked on the tree), `agents/reviews/` (a second agent's read of a diff, when one is used).
- The cockpit: `tools/cockpit`, frozen at 2026-09-10 plus bug fixes; its own roadmap `tools/cockpit/ROADMAP.md` maps every item onto this ledger. It is running on port 8787 from the hub as the dashboard in daily use; an oculus dev server must use another port, and nothing in an oculus package may disturb it.
- The hub data the migration reads: `projects/**/project.yml` (91 manifests on 2026-09-10: 50 in the flat tiers ideas 31, active 4, manuscripts 15; 16 under `coauthor/<category>/`; 25 under `archive/<category>/`; counted with `find projects -name project.yml`), `registries/*.yml`, the collections named in `tools/cockpit/data/config/layout.yml`, and the feeds in hub-root `feeds/` since C12. Never written by an agent.
- Claude Code's own stores, read-only: transcripts `~/.claude/projects/<slug>/<id>.jsonl`; the live registry `~/.claude/sessions/<pid>.json`. No signal to any registry pid other than the liveness probe.
- tmux 3.7c is installed (`/opt/homebrew/bin/tmux`). The hub's `.claude/settings.local.json` allows `git add`, `commit`, `checkout`, `stash`, `branch`, `cherry-pick`, `worktree` and `reset --soft` without a prompt.
- Axel runs oculus with `npm run dev` from the hub's `tools/oculus` until O39 part C moves his server to `~/workspace/oculus` (`vite dev`, pid 15895 on 2026-10-03), serving on 127.0.0.1:8790; the looks are taken on its TEST page. The orchestrator never starts, stops or moves a server on 8790 or 8787; a look that needs a server of its own (D35's port move) runs a scratch oculus from a detached worktree with its own `config/local.yml`, `HOME` and `TMUX_TMPDIR` under the session's scratchpad, its `node_modules` a clone (`cp -cR`), stopped and removed after.

## Procedures, every session

- The verify list, from the root of the checkout it checks: `PATH=$HOME/.nvm/versions/node/v24.21.0/bin:$PATH sh ~/.claude/skills/land/templates/checks.sh /Users/axelfaes/workspace/oculus/.scratch/migration/orchestrator-state.md`. A build on main for a landing is run alone first: `npm run build` from the repository root with the same `PATH`.
- Before any dispatch: the runner lists `ordo-high`, and `printenv CLAUDE_CODE_EFFORT_LEVEL` exits 1. Builders are `ordo-high` on model `opus`, reviewers and brief checks `ordo-high` on model `opus`, each launched with `run_in_background`. After each launch or resume, the served model is read from the agent's transcript: `grep -o '"model":"[^"]*"' ~/.claude/projects/<project folder>/<session id>/subagents/agent-<agent id>.jsonl`, the project folder `-Users-axelfaes-workspace-oculus` for a session started in `~/workspace/oculus`; builders and reviewers must be `claude-opus-5-5`.
- A reviewer's report is the `message` input of the last tool call whose name contains `Handback` in that agent's transcript; it is saved to `agents/reviews/<step>-refuter.md`, a run over a round appended under `## Repair round <n>, refuted`. A builder's report is copied from `.agents/worktrees/<step>/.scratch/migration/agents/reviews/<step>-report.md`, or taken from its final message when it wrote none. Each agent's tokens, tool uses and minutes come from its completion notice's `<usage>` and go into its dispatch entry.
- A run over a repair round reads the round's delta as the files newer than a reference made with `touch -t <the round rulings file's mtime>`, and the reviewer keeps its scratch files in a folder of its own named for the step and round.
- `ListAgents` is run before saying whether any agent is running.
- Refused by the auto-mode classifier, and not pursued by any route: reading `config/local.yml`; navigating to outside console pages (Google Cloud and the like); closing TEST's Account setup tab by JS. Choosing a permission mode or pressing Start in a task composer: refused as "Create Unsafe Agents". Nothing of this kind is attempted (Z551).
- A landing's check run (the gate) starts only while no builder or reviewer of another step is running tests: `ps -Ao command | grep -E 'vitest|svelte-check'` shows no process of another worktree. Otherwise the landing waits for that run to end (Axel, 2026-10-13, after D71's landing met timeouts under the load of D64's builder).
